- Froze the empty `pkglist` cause as a metadata-format incompatibility: accepted `local-source-v3` writes package checksum entries with GNU `md5sum --tag`, so package lines end in the digest instead of the `.txz` path.
- Bound the Slackpkg package-list contract in which `CHECKSUMS.md5` package entries are selected by a terminal Slackware package extension and the final whitespace-delimited field is the package path; the tagged v3 entry is therefore excluded before `pkglist.awk` can produce a row.
- Reproduced the failure repository-only: tagged output produces zero package-filter matches, while ordinary untagged GNU `md5sum` output leaves the package path as the final field and passes the terminal `.txz` filter.

### Fixed

- The Slackware-current recovery path now distinguishes a completed package transaction followed by boot-orchestration failure from an incomplete package update. The exact installed post-state is retained instead of restarting candidate review or reinstalling packages.
- Post-apply boot validation now accepts the unchanged, previously reviewed GRUB menuentry pairing `/boot/vmlinuz-generic` with `/boot/initrd-generic.img` after both links have moved coherently to the exact 6.18.42 artifacts; no unnecessary GRUB mutation is performed.
- Rollback reporting no longer claims that the 6.18.40 disk kernel and initrd survived the package upgrade when only the running session and old module tree remain.
- Slackware-current normal-update apply can now accept either the historical apply-ready boot record or the final transaction-readiness record. The readiness path requires the exact accepted archive and refreshed candidate digests, `readiness_status=apply-ready`, `pause_safe=false`, `apply_authorized=false`, and the explicit authorization-review next stage; stale or falsely pause-safe records fail closed.
- GenInitrd/GRUB ownership sensitive-state capture now references the declared `GENINITRD_SCRIPT` instead of the nonexistent `GENERATOR_SCRIPT`. The ownership harness executes `capture_sensitive_state()` under `set -u`, verifies both declared script paths in the captured inventory, and rejects reintroduction of the undeclared name.
- The GRUB atomic-replacement harness now isolates `PATH` while simulating an unavailable `grub-script-check`, so the fail-closed branch remains deterministic even when the host running the tests has a real validator binary installed.
- The exact Slackware-current kernel-package preflight now binds the accepted `geninitrd-managed-versioned-initrd` boot and chain-restart records instead of the revoked direct-no-initrd records. Before any download it verifies the live generic-kernel hash, named initrd link, versioned-initrd hash and size, GenInitrd symlink policy, active GRUB hash, and same-menuentry kernel/initrd pairing.
- Package-preflight boot immutability now includes `/etc/default/geninitrd`, `/boot/initrd-generic.img`, the versioned initrd, GenInitrd scripts, active hooks, and GRUB state. Evidence-local `grub-mkconfig` output must preserve the generic-kernel and named-initrd pair.
- GenInitrd versioned-initrd validation no longer parses space-separated `stat` output under the script-wide newline/tab-only `IFS`. It uses colon-delimited metadata with a local parser, rejects empty or malformed fields before arithmetic, and avoids shell integer diagnostics while preserving root ownership and non-writable mode requirements.
- Slackware-current boot-layout discovery no longer fails when package-list metadata identifies one exact target kernel but the local Slackpkg file inventory has not yet published the target `vmlinuz` member. It records either `present` or `deferred-to-exact-package-preflight`; the exact downloaded `.txz` remains the mandatory ownership source before apply readiness.
- The kernel-chain restart wrapper now validates and republishes the nested target-image metadata state, corrected `geninitrd-managed-versioned-initrd` mode, versioned-initrd SHA-256, and GenInitrd transition requirement. It rejects the revoked direct-no-initrd baseline and keeps the exact-package preflight as the next stage.
- The Slackware-current boot preflight success message no longer hard-codes the historical 57-candidate count. It now describes the accepted record generically, so restarted chains for later exact candidate sets cannot publish misleading evidence text.
- The reference boot module now distinguishes `mkinitrd-managed`, validated `direct-generic-no-initrd`, and partial layouts. On the direct Slackware-current path, `kernel-generic` changes no longer create an impossible initrd requirement; initrd execution is suppressed, but GRUB remains mandatory and is blocked until the new package-owned `vmlinuz-VERSION` target plus `/lib/modules/VERSION` pass post-update validation.
- Direct generic boot detection now parses exactly one safe `BOOT_IMAGE`, requires an exact GRUB `linux` or `linuxefi` reference rather than a comment or substring, resolves the `/var/log/packages` compatibility path canonically, rejects ambiguous or symlinked package records, and ties both the running kernel image and module tree to one installed `kernel-generic` record.
- Generated GRUB configurations for direct generic transitions must reference either the validated versioned kernel or the validated generic symlink. Syntax-valid but stale configurations are rejected before atomic replacement, and the structured result exposes the preparation layout, direct-kernel validation status, resolved kernel path, module path, and failure reason.
- Slackware-current kernel boot discovery no longer requires an initrd unconditionally. A coherent direct GRUB boot is accepted only when `mkinitrd.conf` and `initrd.gz` are both absent, `/boot/vmlinuz-generic` resolves to the package-owned running `vmlinuz-VERSION`, one exact target package trio exists, and the running `BOOT_IMAGE` is present in a syntax-valid GRUB configuration. Target file inventory is consumed when available and otherwise deferred explicitly to the exact-package preflight. Mixed states still fail closed.
- Slackware-current normal-update apply now requires a matching accepted boot-layout preflight whenever the refreshed candidate set contains kernel packages. `--allow-kernel-update` alone is no longer sufficient: the separately supplied evidence SHA-256 must identify an accepted record for the same candidate digest with `apply_ready=true` and `apply_authorized=false`. Missing, stale, mismatched, or discovery-only evidence blocks before package installation.
- Normal-update candidate extraction now recognizes Slackware package filenames whose architecture field is exactly `x86`, including `kernel-headers`. The 2026-08-03 diagnostic proved that omitting this tag understated the upgrade count, kernel count, and candidate-set SHA-256; such incomplete evidence is now explicitly rejected and a fresh preflight is required.
- Normal-update evidence sidecars now store only the archive basename and the scenario prints a destination-side `sha256sum -c` command, so the copied archive and sidecar verify directly from `/home/promano` instead of referencing the private `/var/tmp` source path.
- Evidence SHA-256 sidecars from the ELILO oldkernel retention preflight now record only the archive basename. After the archive and sidecar are copied together to `/home/promano`, `sha256sum -c` works from that destination without trying to reopen the private source path under `/var/tmp`; the script also prints the exact verification command.
- The ELILO oldkernel retention preflight now resolves the supported Slackware `/var/log/packages` compatibility symlink to its canonical package database before enumeration. It still rejects broken links, non-directory destinations, unreadable databases, package-record symlinks, duplicate retained kernel records, and unsafe package-log paths.
- Package and boot immutability checks now compare only four successfully captured regular files. Missing or partial captures can no longer trigger unconditional `sha256sum` errors or produce false unchanged-state passes from empty hash values.
- Cleanup eligibility is now evaluated only after the final package and boot captures are complete and byte-identical to their initial state. Any capture, comparison, retention, or later-boot failure forces `cleanup_eligible=false` before the cleanup plan is written.
- Replaced the unsupported single extended-regex argument to `slackpkg download` with three exact package-name invocations: `kernel-generic`, `kernel-huge`, and `kernel-modules`. Each raw status is recorded separately, the sequence stops at the first failure, and the existing cache resolver still accepts only the exact reviewed version, architecture, build, and repository path.

- Phase 1 step 64 is prepared as the corrected candidate-chain classification boundary. The refresh wrapper now compares the exact generic, headers, and source transaction instead of treating every digest change as a kernel change; it uses the accepted 69-candidate record by default, verifies configured critical candidates, identifies strict userspace supersets, and exposes whether the corrected kernel evidence may be explicitly rebound only after userspace review. The readiness summary now returns blocked candidate drift to `current-candidate-chain-refresh-preflight` rather than incorrectly naming apply authorization. No package, initrd, DKMS, or GRUB action is available.
### Added

- Added `tests/fixtures/reference/acceptance/normal-update/slackware-current-kernel-post-apply-reboot-review-20260805-accepted.json` as the immutable accepted record for the 13-pass real reboot review, exact evidence digest, code and policy identities, effective GRUB selection, unchanged host state, degraded rollback acknowledgement, and one authorized but not yet executed manual reboot.
- Added `tests/acceptance/reference/test-current-kernel-post-apply-reboot-review.sh`, a read-only Slackware-current boundary that authorizes only a later manual reboot after revalidating the accepted local checkpoint. It rejects package or boot drift, a changed current boot path, GRUB syntax or digest changes, nonempty `next_entry`, ambiguous defaults, and any effective selection that does not pair `/boot/vmlinuz-generic` with `/boot/initrd-generic.img`; it records selector, entry title, and entry ID in structured evidence.
- Added `tests/reference/test-current-kernel-post-apply-reboot-review-harness.sh` with 68 checks covering zero mutation, exact checkpoint and authorization binding, literal defaults, `saved_entry`, generated menuentry IDs, numeric submenu paths, package and artifact drift, GRUB environment overrides, unsupported selectors, degraded rollback acknowledgement, and fail-closed pause-safety separation.
- Added `tests/fixtures/reference/acceptance/normal-update/slackware-current-kernel-post-apply-reboot-review-policy.json`, binding the accepted safe-pause archive, checkpoint, recovery policy and code, reboot-review code, host identity, target 6.18.42, degraded rollback truth, forbidden repository refresh and host mutation, and explicit authorization scope SHA-256 `01739dc15e197ce7a4d15e749ca2e0b844de3900e39be83413ad7e5ecdc2307c`.
- Added `tests/acceptance/reference/test-current-post-package-boot-recovery-verification.sh`, an exact non-mutating Slackware-current post-package boundary that binds the completed 2,040-record transaction, target 6.18.42 kernel/initrd/modules, generic links, restored GenInitrd controls, unchanged accepted GRUB digest, same-menuentry generic pair, and degraded 6.18.40 rollback state before reporting a genuinely safe pause.

Its mandatory preflight asks `slackpkg install-new` and `upgrade-all` for real candidate lists with dialog disabled and a negative default answer, proves that package and boot state remain unchanged, classifies kernel and critical candidates, and creates portable evidence before any installation is authorized.
- The same scenario provides an explicitly gated apply mode that requires an exact hostname acknowledgement, requires the exact reviewed candidate-set SHA-256, separately authorizes kernel and configured critical candidates, isolates Flatpak, SBo, ELF, and Cinnamon, retains boot preparation in `auto` mode, validates successful stable codes `0`, `4`, or `5`, and requires a real package-database change.
- Added `tests/reference/test-normal-update-acceptance-harness.sh` and representative non-interactive `slackpkg` probe fixtures with focused coverage for candidate extraction, right-to-left package-name classification, critical and kernel detection, configuration isolation, physical-host safety gates, evidence summaries, and the one-line `promano` copy fallback.
- Added a sanitized accepted preflight record for the physical Slackware-current normal-update scenario, including the evidence digest, tested platform, exact ten-package candidate set, unchanged package and boot state, and assertion totals.
- Added `tests/fixtures/reference/acceptance/normal-update/slackware-current-apply-reviewed.json` to preserve the reviewed physical-host transaction, its archive digest, exact package changes, boot-state result, 27-file `.new` observation, and explicit revalidation gate for the hardened reference.
- Added sanitized accepted Slackware 15.0 normal-update preflight and apply records. They preserve both evidence digests, the exact 196-package candidate digest, the 12 `install-new` and 184 `upgrade-all` candidates, five critical candidates, temporary deferral of three boot-kernel packages, package-record growth from 1,554 to 1,566, stable code `4`, unchanged boot state, and the real-system inventory of 45 deferred `.new` files.
- Added `tests/reference/test-slackpkg-postinstall-policy.sh` with focused coverage for `-postinst=off`, exact update/install-new/upgrade-all argument boundaries, deterministic `/etc/*.new` enumeration, human and structured warnings, JSON metadata, and scan failures.
- The normal-update harness now refreshes Slackware metadata before every candidate classification, records the raw refresh status and output, and repeats that boundary immediately before real apply so newly published packages cannot bypass review through stale local lists: any candidate-set digest change blocks apply, and kernel plus configured critical packages retain separate authorization gates.

- Added `tests/acceptance/reference/test-no-updates.sh` as the first real-system Phase 1 acceptance scenario. It requires an explicit apply acknowledgement, verifies the declared Slackware target, proves the no-updates precondition with the real `--check --json` path, runs the isolated real `--apply --json` Slackware workflow, compares byte-level installed-package evidence and selected boot state, and creates a private evidence archive with a SHA-256 sidecar.
- Added `tests/acceptance/reference/README.md` with the safety boundary, execution commands for Slackware 15.0 and Slackware-current, scenario scope, expected result contract, and evidence-sanitization guidance.
- Added expected provisional check and apply fixtures under `tests/fixtures/reference/acceptance/no-updates/` for stable exit code `0`, no reboot guidance, valid unchanged package snapshots, disabled optional modules, and empty warning/error arrays.
- Added sanitized accepted-evidence records for both mandatory targets under `tests/fixtures/reference/acceptance/no-updates/`, including archive digests, tested platforms, raw `slackpkg` statuses, package counts, unchanged boot state, and assertion totals.
- Added `tests/reference/test-no-updates-acceptance-harness.sh` with focused coverage for target validation, explicit apply confirmation, check-before-apply ordering, isolated configuration generation, fixture validation and rejection, deterministic package evidence, actionable structured-failure details, archive contents, caller ownership, private publication modes, and SHA-256 verification.

- Added `tests/reference/test-cron-minimal-environment.sh` with focused coverage for direct and harnessed `env -i` execution, hostile inherited values, deterministic administrative command discovery, detached standard streams, `/dev/null` input, human/JSON/NDJSON output separation, non-interactive `slackpkg` arguments, prompt suppression, and owner-only runtime files.

- Added `tests/reference/test-error-exit-codes.sh` with focused coverage for all stable process statuses `0` through `8`, every current structured-result error source, successful non-zero reboot outcomes, early argument/configuration/privilege/lock failures, runtime and logging initialization failures, completion-timestamp failures, and JSON/NDJSON exit-code propagation.

- Added `tests/reference/test-signal-cleanup.sh` with real subprocess coverage for `SIGHUP`, `SIGINT`, and `SIGTERM`, conventional interruption statuses, active-lock observation, explicit lock release, owned temporary-resource cleanup, unrelated-file preservation, pre-runtime interruption safety, non-resumption, and source-level trap-order guards.
- Added dedicated runtime trap installation, interruption handling, and instance-lock release helpers.

- Added `tests/reference/test-grub-atomic-replacement.sh` with focused coverage for temporary same-directory generation, syntax validation, active-file preservation, permission retention, missing validators, symlink rejection, concurrent modification detection, failed atomic replacement, cleanup boundaries, source audits, and provisional JSON reporting.
- Added reusable GRUB transaction helpers for active-configuration fingerprinting, owner-only temporary-file preparation, `grub-script-check` validation, guarded cleanup, and same-directory atomic installation.

- Added `tests/reference/test-grub-blocked-after-initrd-failure.sh` with focused coverage for initrd validation failures, failed regeneration, missing initrd applicability, GRUB-only work, command-attempt boundaries, event suppression, provisional JSON, result diagnostics, and successful continuation after a valid initrd.
- Added a reusable boot-preparation coordinator and an independent GRUB prerequisite guard that require every scheduled initrd action to complete successfully before `grub-mkconfig` can be attempted.
- Added `tests/reference/test-initrd-installed-kernel.sh` with focused coverage for safe `mkinitrd.conf` parsing, post-update kernel-package resolution, stale and missing `KERNEL_VERSION`, installed module trees, output-path selection, apply-time command guards, schema defaults, source audits, and provisional JSON reporting.
- Added reusable initrd validation helpers that resolve the installed kernel from the validated post-update package snapshot, parse scalar `mkinitrd.conf` assignments without evaluation, verify the matching modules directory, and expose stable diagnostics.
- Added `tests/reference/test-elf-architecture-resolution.sh` with focused coverage for ELF32, ELF64, x86, x86-64, AArch64, endianness, exact soname matching, mixed-architecture loader caches, initial scans, post-rebuild verification, source audits, and provisional JSON metadata.
- Added normalized static ELF identity extraction and compatibility helpers that compare class, data encoding, and machine without executing either inspected objects or cached libraries.
- Added `tests/reference/test-elf-static-inspection.sh` with guarded executable fixtures, exact `DT_NEEDED` matching, symlink handling, parser-failure behavior, post-rebuild verification, fail-closed cache refresh, source audits, and provisional JSON assertions that prove inspected objects are never invoked.
- Added reusable static ELF helpers for magic-byte filtering, canonical regular-file resolution, `readelf -d` dependency extraction, `readelf -h` identity extraction, one-pass `ldconfig -p` cache normalization, exact architecture-compatible soname lookup, diagnostics, and post-rebuild reinspection.
- Added `tests/reference/test-sbo-personal-queue-protection.sh` with focused coverage for queue-directory resolution, private regular-file copies, nested queues, ignored symlinks, canonical path separation, successful and failed `sqg` isolation, apply-time source selection, cleanup boundaries, and provisional JSON reporting.
- Added reusable helpers that resolve direct, official default-value, and local sbopkg queue-directory assignments; prepare an owner-only per-run queue workspace without following personal queue symlinks; and generate private system/local `sqg` configuration wrappers.
- Added `tests/reference/test-sbo-options.sh` with focused coverage for safe option parsing, quoted values, queue option collection, persistent overrides, duplicate and conflict rejection, atomic failure behavior, ABI/ELF target option application, configuration defaults, provisional output, and final `sbopkg -b -B` submission.
- Added schema-1 `sbo.options_file` configuration with the backward-compatible default `/etc/slack-update/sbo-options.sqf` and normalized per-package SBo option records.
- Added reusable SBo build-option parsing, normalization, collection, rendering, and structured-output helpers.
- Added `tests/reference/test-sbo-dependency-order.sh` with focused coverage for dependency constraints, shared dependencies, deterministic topological ordering, filesystem-order independence, cyclic and invalid queue rejection, atomic replacement, final queue merging, and `sbopkg -b -B` submission.
- Added reusable SBo queue-graph helpers that combine generated queue constraints and produce one deterministic dependency order.
- Added `tests/reference/test-sbo-target-selection.sh` with focused coverage for active queue records, build-option syntax, recursive references, deselected records, unsafe names, C-locale normalization, filesystem-order independence, exact installed SBo detection, broken-object ownership, atomic failure behavior, and final target submission.
- Added reusable SBo target-selection helpers for queue records, installed SBo packages, and deterministic target-set merging.
- Added `tests/reference/test-partial-slackware-update.sh` with focused coverage for failures in `slackpkg update`, `install-new`, and `upgrade-all`, post-failure snapshot capture, blocked module state, event suppression, provisional JSON reporting, and the successful continuation path.
- Added validated package snapshot capture with canonical normalization, deterministic ordering, duplicate rejection, record counts, and atomic replacement.
- Added `tests/reference/test-package-snapshots.sh` with focused coverage for valid, empty, missing, malformed, unsorted, duplicate, and non-canonical snapshots, plus apply-workflow guards.
- Added a reusable Slackware package-record parser that extracts name, version, architecture, and build from the rightmost fields used by `pkgtools`.
- Added `tests/reference/test-package-name-parsing.sh` and representative Slackware 15.0, Slackware-current, patched-package, multi-hyphen, plus-sign, and SBo fixture records.
- Added stable process exit codes `0` through `8`, including dedicated statuses for partial updates, unsafe boot preparation, successful reboot guidance, concurrent execution, invalid input, and unavailable privilege.
- Added stable exit-code descriptions to human-readable summaries and final JSON results.
- Added `enabled`, `disabled`, and `auto` activation modes for the optional Flatpak, SBo, ELF, Cinnamon, and boot modules.
- Added explicit requirement and applicability probes for every optional module, with activation state and reason reporting in human-readable, JSON, and event output.
- Added schema-1 compatibility defaults so configurations created before module modes were introduced continue to load with omitted modes interpreted as `auto`.
- Added `data/config/slack-update.conf` as the schema-versioned system configuration for the shell reference.
- Added a strict, non-evaluating INI-style parser that rejects unknown sections and keys, duplicates, missing values, invalid booleans, unsafe paths and tokens, and unsupported schema versions.
- Added Slackware 15.0 and Slackware-current as explicit, mandatory compatibility targets in the roadmap and contribution policy.
- Added `--check` as a non-destructive Slackware repository update check using `slackpkg check-updates`.
- Added `--apply` as an explicit selector for the existing update workflow.
- Added `--dry-run` to inspect the current host state and print the complete apply sequence, conditional triggers, current SBo queues, ABI rebuild candidates, ELF findings, optional-component availability, and boot preparation actions without executing update, synchronization, build, installation, initrd, or bootloader commands.
- Added `--json` for one provisional structured result document on standard output.
- Added `--events` for provisional newline-delimited JSON progress events covering operation, module, action, warning, error, and completion states.
- Added a C-oriented `.gitignore` covering prospective Meson and CMake build output, compiler artifacts, test output, and common editor files.
- Added `CONTRIBUTING.md` with the English-language policy, phase-gate rules, reference-script validation requirements, and commit-message convention.
- Added the GNU General Public License version 3, with the project designated as `GPL-3.0-or-later` in the README.
- Added this changelog using the Keep a Changelog structure.

### Fixed

- The GRUB atomic-replacement harness now isolates `PATH` while simulating an unavailable `grub-script-check`, so the fail-closed branch remains deterministic even when the host running the tests has a real validator binary installed.
- Slackware-current kernel boot discovery no longer requires an initrd unconditionally. A coherent direct GRUB boot is accepted only when `mkinitrd.conf` and `initrd.gz` are both absent, `/boot/vmlinuz-generic` resolves to the package-owned `vmlinuz-VERSION`, repository metadata contains the target versioned image, and the running `BOOT_IMAGE` is present in a syntax-valid GRUB configuration. Mixed states still fail closed.
- Package-database enumeration now follows only the command-line compatibility symlink with `find -H`, so Slackware 15.0 and Slackware-current installations whose `/var/log/packages` points to the real `pkgtools` database produce valid snapshots, installed-SBo target sets, and exact package-name lookups without following symlinks stored inside the database.
- No-updates host metadata now records the configured and resolved package-database paths and follows the compatibility symlink when listing installed `slackpkg` records.
- The no-updates acceptance scenario now invokes the readable reference script through `bash` after syntax validation instead of requiring its executable bit to survive ZIP extraction or shared-folder copying.
- Default evidence parent directories are published with traversable permissions, while the `.tar.gz` archive and SHA-256 sidecar are assigned to the original `sudo` caller with mode `0600`; the expanded evidence directory remains root-only.
- Failed structured check or apply validation now prints the stable exit code, each Slackware command status, and the structured error list before archiving evidence.

### Changed

- Normal-update apply validation now distinguishes the broad `kernel_changes` package-group indicator from explicit boot requirements. Changes to `kernel-firmware`, `kernel-source`, or headers may report `kernel_changes=true` without requiring stable code `5`; only `initrd_required` or `grub_required` mandates successful boot preparation and reboot-required status, while critical userspace updates without boot work correctly use stable code `4`.
- Unattended `slackpkg install-new` and `upgrade-all` now use `-postinst=off`; the reference preserves active configuration files, never supplies the generic batch answer `y` to the `K/O/R/P` new-configuration menu, inventories pending regular `/etc/*.new` files after the package transaction, and exposes the deferred policy, scan validity, count, paths, and warnings in human and JSON results.
- Normal-update and no-updates acceptance validators now require the explicit deferred post-install policy and validate pending `.new` metadata without treating a non-empty list as a package-transaction failure.
- The real-system acceptance documentation now records accepted no-updates results for Slackware 15.0 and Slackware-current, points to both sanitized evidence fixtures, and documents a single-line `install` fallback that avoids invisible paragraph separators when copying evidence into the `promano` acceptance account.
- The reference now normalizes its root execution environment before argument parsing: `PATH` includes Slackware administrative directories, identity and temporary-directory variables use deterministic root values, locale and terminal behavior are non-interactive, desktop-session variables are removed, Git/SSH credential prompts are disabled, and `umask 077` protects newly created runtime state.
- The documented cron example now uses explicit `--check` rather than the backward-compatible implicit apply operation.

- Runtime initialization now fails closed when start-time capture, runtime-directory creation, temporary-file creation, dry-run workspace creation, or dry-run state-file creation fails; the selected operation is not started and the process returns stable code `1`.
- Logging setup now verifies required commands and writability before redirecting output, and completion-time capture failure is folded into the final non-zero result instead of being silently ignored.

- Runtime traps are now installed immediately after successful lock acquisition, before runtime initialization creates directories or temporary files.
- `SIGHUP`, `SIGINT`, and `SIGTERM` now disable recursive traps, run ownership-aware cleanup, release and close the instance lock, and terminate with statuses `129`, `130`, and `143` instead of returning to the interrupted workflow.
- Failed lock acquisition now closes file descriptor 9 before returning the stable concurrent-execution status.

- `grub-mkconfig` now writes only to an unpredictable owner-only temporary file beside the active configuration; the active `grub.cfg` path is never passed to the generator.
- Generated GRUB configuration must be a readable, non-empty regular file and pass `grub-script-check` before replacement; generator, validator, and install failures preserve the active file.
- GRUB replacement now rejects active symlinks and configurations outside the configured GRUB directory, detects concurrent active-file changes by fingerprint, preserves existing ownership and permissions, and uses a same-directory `mv -T` for atomic installation.
- Boot probing now requires both `grub-mkconfig` and `grub-script-check`, dry-run documents the staged transaction, cleanup removes only owned regular temporary files, and provisional JSON exposes generation, validation, installation, and replacement state.

- GRUB configuration generation is now blocked before its action-start event when required initrd validation or regeneration fails; `update_grub_configuration` repeats the same guard defensively at the command boundary.
- Required but unavailable initrd preparation in auto mode now blocks a simultaneously scheduled GRUB update instead of permitting bootloader generation without a validated initrd.
- Provisional JSON and human-readable diagnostics now distinguish a deliberate `grub_state=blocked` safety outcome from an attempted `grub-mkconfig` failure and report whether the GRUB command was ever invoked.
- Initrd regeneration now derives the target version from the installed `kernel-generic` package in the validated post-update snapshot, requires an exact `KERNEL_VERSION` match, verifies `/lib/modules/<version>`, and refuses to invoke `mkinitrd` when any check fails.
- Boot probing now treats an installed `mkinitrd` command plus a regular configuration file as an applicable validation path, so malformed or stale configuration fails closed during a triggered update instead of being silently skipped in auto mode.
- Initrd output validation now recognizes `OUTPUT_IMAGE`, retains compatibility with `OUTPUT`, and otherwise uses the configured default path; apply and dry-run JSON identify the package snapshot as the kernel-version source.
- Static loader-cache records now retain the resolved library path together with its ELF class, data encoding, and machine; a `DT_NEEDED` entry is satisfied only by an exact soname record whose three identity fields match the inspected object.
- Initial broken-object detection and post-`sbopkg` verification now use the same architecture-aware compatibility check, so an ELF32, x86-64, AArch64, or opposite-endian library cannot satisfy an incompatible object merely because the soname matches.
- Provisional apply and dry-run JSON now report `architecture_specific_resolution=true`, the exact `class`, `data`, and `machine` match fields, and the number of usable architecture-tagged cache records.
- Initial broken-object detection and post-`sbopkg` verification now share the same static `readelf` plus loader-cache path; the former dynamic-loader verification command has been removed, inspected objects are never executed, and unreadable objects remain unresolved during post-build verification.
- ELF scans now build an owner-only architecture-tagged library cache once per inspection, reject empty cache results, require exact soname and ELF-identity matches rather than substring or cross-architecture matches, skip non-ELF and non-regular targets before `readelf`, and fail the module when static cache preparation fails.
- Provisional apply and dry-run JSON now report `inspection_method=readelf+ldconfig-cache`, `executes_inspected_objects=false`, and the static scan and verification statuses.
- `sqg -a` now receives an owner-only `SBOPKG_CONF` wrapper that loads the original system and local configurations and then reasserts Slack-Update's disposable `QUEUEDIR` after both layers; the configured personal queue directory is never passed to the generator or modified by queue regeneration.
- Failed workspace preparation or a non-zero `sqg` result now blocks SBo target construction and final queue submission instead of falling back to personal queues or consuming a partially generated workspace.
- Apply-time SBo target, dependency, and build-option collection now reads the private queue copy, while dry-run continues to inspect the configured personal queues without modifying them.
- Private queue workspace preparation now fails closed for relative paths, symlinked queue roots, pre-existing destinations, or destinations equal to or canonically inside personal queue state; regular `.sqf` files are copied with modes `0600` under a `0700` workspace and queue symlinks are ignored.
- Cleanup now removes only the generated queue workspace whose owned path and canonical identity were recorded at creation; rejected pre-existing paths and later symlink replacements are left untouched.
- Provisional SBo JSON now distinguishes the personal queue directory from the generated workspace and reports isolation, copied-file, and ignored-symlink metadata.
- Final SBo queues now preserve safe per-package `NAME=value` build options while retaining dependency order and deterministic ABI/ELF target appending.
- Options present in current generated queues are deduplicated and checked for conflicts, while the optional persistent `sbo.options_file` takes precedence so custom choices survive `sqg -a` regeneration.
- Invalid, unsafe, duplicate, or contradictory SBo option records now fail atomically, preserve previous queue state, block `sbopkg`, and appear in dry-run and provisional JSON reporting.
- Generated SBo queue records are now combined as dependency constraints and resolved with a deterministic topological order instead of being alphabetically reordered.
- Shared SBo dependencies now appear once, independent packages use a C-locale tie break, and cyclic or contradictory queue constraints fail atomically before `sbopkg` runs.
- Final SBo queue construction now preserves the dependency-ordered core and appends unique ABI and broken-ELF targets in deterministic order.
- SBo target selection now ignores comments, recursive queue references, and deselected records, extracts package names independently from build options, rejects unsafe names, and produces a C-locale sorted unique target set independent of queue-file enumeration order.
- ABI rebuild candidates and broken-object SBo owners now share the same canonical target validation and deterministic target-set merge rules.
- Invalid SBo target selection now fails the SBo module, is exposed in provisional JSON, and blocks queue submission to `sbopkg`.
- Apply now stops after the validated post-update snapshot when any Slackware package operation fails, preventing Flatpak, package-change analysis, SBo, ELF, Cinnamon, initrd, and GRUB work from running against a partial Slackware state.
- Partial Slackware updates now mark every secondary module as `blocked`, include a stable blocking reason in summaries and provisional JSON, and emit no secondary-module start events.
- Package snapshots now fail closed when the package database cannot be enumerated or validated instead of silently creating incomplete state with `|| true`.
- Apply now stops before invoking `slackpkg` when the baseline snapshot is invalid and stops snapshot-dependent follow-up work when the post-update snapshot is invalid.
- Apply summaries and provisional JSON results now report snapshot validity, package counts, and validation errors for the before and after states.
- Replaced prefix-based package snapshot matching with literal parsed package-name comparison, preventing collisions such as `openssl` versus `openssl-solibs`.
- Replaced `rev | cut` SBo-name extraction and arbitrary tag filtering with parsed build-suffix checks for dry-run candidates, ABI rebuild targets, and broken-object ownership.
- Cinnamon package detection now compares the parsed package name exactly instead of relying on a filename glob.
- The reference script can now be sourced by regression tests without invoking `main()`; direct execution remains unchanged.
- Final JSON results now set `exit_code_stable` to `true`, include `exit_code_meaning`, and preserve `success=true` for successful reboot-recommended (`4`) and reboot-required (`5`) outcomes.
- The final `operation_completed` NDJSON event now carries the stable process exit code; intermediate action events continue to expose raw external-command statuses.
- Invalid arguments and configuration now return `7`, missing privilege returns `8`, and a concurrent instance returns `6` before workflow execution begins.
- Apply-result precedence is now unsafe boot (`3`), partial or verification failure (`2`), reboot required (`5`), reboot recommended (`4`), then success without reboot (`0`).
- Kernel headers changes alone no longer produce a reboot-required result; code `5` is tied to kernel image or module changes that schedule boot preparation.
- Optional modules now execute only when permitted by their configured activation mode.
- `enabled` reports missing module requirements as errors, `disabled` bypasses probing and execution, and `auto` treats unavailable or irrelevant optional software as non-fatal.
- Cinnamon graphical ABI triggers are ignored when the Cinnamon module is disabled or not applicable.
- Boot auto mode independently selects validated initrd and GRUB preparation paths after kernel changes; disabled mode suppresses both actions, while enabled mode retains strict failure reporting.
- Provisional JSON results now include each optional module's configured mode, activation state, reason, and operation-specific final state.
- Moved runtime paths, log retention, package groups, SBo and boot paths, Cinnamon repository settings, ELF scan roots, and the Slackware `install-new` and `upgrade-all` decisions out of the script and into the configuration file.
- Kept executable command names and argument structures fixed in the reference script so configuration values cannot introduce arbitrary shell execution.
- Added the effective configuration path to human-readable summaries and provisional final JSON results.
- Renamed the reference script installation target, lock file, work directory, log directory, and runtime identity from `sbo-auto` to `slack-update`, without changing the update workflow.
- Split the linear reference workflow into 24 clearly named functions, including the `main()` coordinator, while preserving command order and observable behavior.
- Added two command-line interface functions for `-h` and `--help`, explicit rejection of unknown options and unexpected positional arguments, and no operational mode changes; the script then contained 26 named functions.
- Added mutually exclusive operation parsing for `--check`, `--apply`, and `--dry-run`; running without an operation remains a compatibility alias for apply.
- Added an isolated temporary workspace for dry-run queue and ELF inspection data so planning does not overwrite the persistent reference state under `/var/lib/slack-update`; normal locking and logging remain enabled.
- Reserved standard output for machine-readable data when either `--json` or `--events` is selected, while human-readable progress continues through standard error and the normal log.
- Made `--json` and `--events` mutually exclusive so each output stream remains unambiguous and directly parseable.
- Instrumented the existing check, dry-run, and apply coordinators with provisional operation, module, and action events without changing their human-readable execution order.
- Relocated the existing shell implementation to `tools/reference/slack-update-reference.sh` without changing its contents.
- Updated the roadmap to reflect the repository work completed in Phase 0.

### Development context

- Current roadmap phase: **Phase 1 — Stabilize and validate the shell reference**.
- Current continuation boundary: the step-81 reboot review is accepted with archive SHA-256 `e41138a31225a795c65ddf76fa75fde19072f5892c9719af32d06b564027937b`. The machine still runs 6.18.40, the exact generic kernel/initrd pair targets 6.18.42, `pause_safe=true`, and one manual reboot is authorized but not yet executed. Do not refresh Slackpkg metadata or repeat any candidate, payload, readiness, apply, recovery, or reboot-review stage. Reboot once, then run the separate `current-kernel-post-reboot-verification` boundary before closing the update.
- Phase 0 is complete, committed as `3064cfa`, tagged as `planning-v1`, and published to GitHub.
- The first twenty-six Phase 1 refactoring, interface, parsing, and safety tasks are complete: runtime identifiers use `slack-update`, the script is split into named functions, argument parsing is available, `--check`, `--apply`, and `--dry-run` are implemented, `--json` emits a final structured result, `--events` streams machine-readable progress, operational settings are loaded from a validated configuration file, optional modules implement `enabled`, `disabled`, and `auto`, process exit codes `0` through `8` are stable, every represented error outcome is verified to produce a non-zero process result, and detached cron-style execution is validated under a normalized minimal environment.
- Phase 1 real-system acceptance began with a reproducible no-updates scenario, expected structured fixtures, evidence capture, and automated harness validation. Both mandatory targets passed on 2026-07-28. Slackware 15.0 preserved 1,594 package records with reviewed archive SHA-256 `5a784cd6d830ac271cc3aad02ed89f2e00c2afd63c88f90aabb74b0a81b0b20b`; Slackware-current preserved 2,035 package records with reviewed archive SHA-256 `ba0c1264d57df5acf6bee843391113327736683ede36ec3d708d58ca174a2976`. Both runs returned stable code `0` for check and apply, preserved raw `slackpkg` no-package statuses `20`, left the observed initrd and GRUB state unchanged, and passed all six scenario assertions.
- The physical Slackware-current normal-update transaction was reviewed on 2026-07-29 with archive SHA-256 `00679a69d9c40033db74b4a73525651a42d0d72b293ec536d1181e87e2ab7e66`. It refreshed and matched the reviewed ten-package candidate digest, returned stable code `0`, upgraded exactly those ten packages, retained 2,039 installed-package records, detected an ABI change through PipeWire, and left kernel, initrd, and GRUB state unchanged. Its nine scenario assertions passed. The evidence also showed 27 `.new` files reaching slackpkg's interactive post-install menu with the global answer `y`; the package transaction is accepted, but the current `-postinst=off` plus explicit inventory policy requires a later real-update revalidation before the Slackware-current row is closed.
- Slackware 15.0 normal-update preflight and apply were reviewed on 2026-08-01. After three boot-kernel packages were temporarily deferred, preflight archive SHA-256 `4774070e7a9173f6486d560e54d7efce8580b76a92449e1698e7449d8557e73c` approved exact candidate-set SHA-256 `baaf89bb3e61662d7bbb10223e2c26b9adc98c443eacdf8273319a6818951410` with 12 new packages, 184 upgrades, five critical candidates, and no boot-kernel candidates. Apply archive SHA-256 `c670a5077f9efb5d64470b46b537754634913c0f1deccd4fcef707c9385339ed` returned stable code `4`, installed or changed all 196 candidates, moved the package database from 1,554 to 1,566 records, preserved initrd and GRUB state, and reported 45 deferred `.new` files without invoking Slackpkg's interactive post-install menu. The observed one-assertion failure was a validator false negative caused by conflating broad kernel-related changes (`kernel-firmware` and `kernel-source`) with boot-kernel replacement; the corrected contract accepts the evidence.
- The script currently contains 164 named functions.
- Running without an operation still selects apply for backward compatibility with the original reference behavior.
- `--json` and `--events` both retain provisional schema version `0`; their complete schemas are not stable APIs yet. The final process exit code is stable: `--json` marks it with `exit_code_stable=true`, and the final `operation_completed` event uses the same code. Intermediate event exit codes remain raw external-command statuses.
- Machine-readable modes reserve standard output. Human-readable progress and external-command output are written to standard error and the normal log.
- `--json` and `--events` are mutually exclusive. The current event types are `operation_started`, `module_started`, `action_started`, `action_completed`, `module_completed`, `warning`, `error`, and `operation_completed`.
- Dry-run uses `slackpkg check-updates` as its only Slackware command and otherwise performs local state inspection. It does not refresh package metadata, so exact changed package names and package-derived ABI or kernel triggers remain conditional until apply compares its before and after snapshots.
